Greater China Technology Hardware  | Asia Pacific
GB200 and GB300 NVL72
Racks in September 2026;
Introduce 2027 Rack Forecast
We provide monthly/quarterly rack shipment forecasts for
ODMs. Within the major Server ODMs, our order of preference
is Wistron  > Hon Hai > Quanta, based on upside to PT.

Greater China Technology Hardware
Asia Pacific
Industry View In-Line
We introduce 2027 forecast at 90.4K racks (+10% y/y) vs our 2026 forecast now
at 82K racks: This year is a strong one for downstream rack assemblers, with rack

shipments up ~209% y/y, but we expect rack shipments to grow another ~10% y/y
to reach 90.4K in CY27. For CY27, our forecast implies Hon Hai will be a share gainer
with rack share up to 43% (from 37% in CY26), with Quanta staying unchanged at
26% and Wistron's share up slightly to 24% (from 23% in CY26).
Quanta (2382.TW, OW): September revenue was NT$325.3B (-23% m/m, +77% y/y).
We attribute the m/m decline mainly to lower GB200/300 rack shipments at 1.55-

1.60K racks (-38% m/m), despite being offset slightly by higher NB shipments at
4.2M units (+62% m/m, -9% y/y). In 3Q, Quanta shipped ~6.2K racks (+3% q/q),
which came in slightly lower than our forecast of ~6.4K racks. Total 3Q revenue
reached ~NT$1,116M (+8% q/q, +125% y/y), which is 15% above MSe but in-line with
the Street. For 2026, we forecast Quanta to ship 20.9K racks, and we introduce our
preliminary 2027 forecast at 23.5K (+12% y/y).
Wistron (3231.TW, OW): September revenue was  NT$380.8B (-17% m/m, +87% y/

y). We attribute the m/m decline to: 1) lower GB200/300 server computing tray
shipments  (1.25-1.30K rack equivalents, by our estimates, down approximately 46%
m/m), despite higher Wiwynn revenue at ~NT$172.6B (+20% m/m, +100% y/y) and
higher NB shipments at 1.5M units (+7% m/m, -32% y/y), flat DT shipments at 500K
(flat m/m, -44% y/y), and higher monitor shipments at 950K (+27% m/m, +6% y/y).
In 3Q, Wistron shipped ~4.8K racks (+22% q/q), which came in slightly lower than

our forecast of ~5.1K racks. Total 3Q revenue reached ~NT$1,149M (+28% q/q,
+102% y/y), which is 10% above MSe and 9% above the Street. For 2026, we
forecast Wistron to ship 19.13K racks, and we introduce our preliminary 2027
forecast at 22K (+15% y/y).
Morgan Stanley does and seeks to do business with
companies covered in Morgan Stanley Research. As a result,
investors should be aware that the firm may have a conflict of
interest that could affect the objectivity of Morgan Stanley

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.
Key Takeaways
We estimate GB200/300 rack output in September  at ~6.5K (-23% m/m).
Quanta shipped 1.55-1.60K  GB200/GB300 racks.
Wistron shipped 1.25-1.30K GB200/GB300 racks.
Hon Hai shipped ~2.7K GB200/300 racks.
We also introduce our 2027 forecast at 90.4K racks (+10% y/y).
October 12, 2026 01:46 PM GMT

### Page 2

M
Idea
2
Hon Hai (2317.TW, OW): September revenue was NT$1,158.6B (+26% m/m, +38% y/
y), with consumer segment revenue seeing the strongest m/m growth, followed by
components and other products, cloud and networking products, and lastly
computing. We model HH's GB200/300 rack shipments at 2.7K (+4% m/m). This
brings total 3Q rack shipments at 7.8K racks (+1% q/q). For 2026, we forecast
Foxconn to ship 30.3K racks, and we introduce our preliminary 2027 forecast at

38.8K (+28% y/y).

Morgan Stanley Research 5
Valuation Methodology and Risks
Hon Hai Precision (2317.TW)
Base case scenario value, derived from a residual income valuation model. Our key assump-
tions include a cost of equity of 8.5%, a medium-term growth rate of 13%, and a terminal
growth rate of 3%.
Risks to Upside
n Better-than-expected iPhone sell-through
n Faster progress in AI server business
n Faster EV business development progress
n Any new M&A activity that could improve sentiment

Risks to Downside
n Lower iPhone sell-through
n Smaller profit contribution from AI business
n Slower EV business development progress
n Geopolitical developments that could negatively affect foreign investment
Quanta Computer Inc. (2382.TW)
Base case, residual income model. Key assumptions include a cost of equity of 9.0% (beta of
1.2, equity premium of 6.0% and risk-free rate of 1.5%), an 8.5% medium-term growth rate, and
a 3% terminal growth rate.

Risks to Upside
n Stronger-than-expected NB demand
n Stronger-than-expected Apple Watch demand
n Stronger-than-expected server demand
n Faster-than-expected AI server penetration
Risks to Downside
n Weaker-than-expected NB demand
n Softer-than-expected Apple Watch demand
n Weak margin performance owing to rising labor costs and sales shortfalls
n Fierce price competition in the mega data center segment
n Slower-than-expected AI server penetration

Wistron Corporation (3231.TW)
Base case, residual income valuation. Key assumptions: 8.7% cost of equity, 7.0% medium-
term growth rate and 3% terminal growth rate.
Risks to Upside
n Faster-than-expected divestiture of consumer electronics business
n Stronger-than-expected NB demand
n Margin expansion from better product mix

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

## Document 102: 261013_ubs_alchip

Source PDF: all report pdf/261013_ubs_alchip.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-13
Pages: 13
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

ab 12 October 2026
Global Research
First Read
Alchip Technologies
Better allocation into 2027E; N2 project on track
Q3 sales up 243% QoQ on Trainium 3 ramp
Alchip reported September sales of NT$9,979 mn, up 14% MoM and 496% YoY,
concluding Q326 sales of NT$26,188 mn for +243% QoQ and 299% YoY, slightly
ahead of consensus, although below UBSe as we assumed a higher wafer allocation for
the Trainium 3 project, while sales contribution for its Chinese automotive customer and

NRE might also have came in lower than our expectation. We now expect Alchip's Q326
GM to arrive at 20.1%, down from 34.8% in Q226 on less favourable mix, leading to
OPM of 15.9%. We forecast Q326 EPS to grow 132% QoQ to NT$46.34.
Trainium 3  to sustain longer with potential upside; Trainium 4 tape-out on track
We expect Alchip's Trainium 3 monthly shipment run-rate to retain at current level into
H127F, and could extend into H227F with potential better allocation for Alchip with a

similar margin profile, subject to the final negotiation with its customer. We think
Trainium 3 deployments could stretch a little bit longer with a limited gap (vs prior ~3
months) before the next-generation Trainium 4 launch given the 3nm capacity tightness.
We continue to forecast overall Trainium 3 shipments at ~1.8mn units for 2026 and will
increase to ~2.8 mn units in 2027E, up 55% YoY, although Alchip's Trainium 3 shipment
could outgrow at 80%+ YoY given potential better allocation. For the Trainium 4 project

(2nm node), management noted it remains on track for tape-out before year-end and
still targets mass production by late-2027 or early 2028. Alchip continues to believe it
could enjoy a higher value-add given the increasing design complexity on chiplet and
higher wafer pricing for the 2nm node, while GM could retain at a similar level
compared with the current N3 project.
Engaging new customers for diversification
Alchip noted it remains in talks with another major CSP customer for potential adoption

of COT (customer-owned tooling) business model in the future for further cost saving.
We believe Alchip's competitive pricing,  strength for faster turnaround, and advanced
packaging knowledge put Alchip in a  good position to capture this opportunity, if the
customer decides to change the business model. Nevertheless, we do not expect
meaningful revenue contribution in 2027-28E from the new CSP customer. On the other
hand, we believe Alchip is in talks with other HPC customers for mid-size physical design

projects and this is likely to become a new revenue stream from 2027E. We estimate it
could book US$200-300mn revenue in 2027E from the new HPC customer.
Valuation: Maintain Buy with NT$6,600 price target
We lower our 2026E EPS by 11% primarily on lower Trainium 3 and automotive
contribution. We raise 2027E/28E EPS by 2%/5% to reflect  revenue contribution from
new HPC customer, offset by higher opex. We keep our PT unchanged at NT$6,600,
based on the same 30x 2027E EPS (vs. a 15-50x PE range). Stock is trading at ~20x 2027

P/E, below its peers and past five year average of 30x.

Company Description
Alchip was founded in 2002 by a group of Cadence managers with 20+ years of experience in
the semiconductor industry. It is an IC design and production service provider, and
management believes it can leverage third-party IP to enable customized chipset solutions for

customers cost effectively and fast to market. The company was funded by Cisco Investment
and Softbank Investment with VC funding in early 2000, then had its IPO on the emerging
board in Taiwan in 2010 and the main board in 2014.
Valuation Method and Risk Statement
We value Alchip using PE methodology.
Downside risks include slower-than-expected advanced node project diversification and
ramp, and growing competition in advanced projects from other IC design service companies.

KEY DEFINITIONS:Forecast Stock Return (FSR) is defined as expected percentage price appreciation plus gross dividend yield over
the next 12 months. In some cases, this yield may be based on accrued dividends. Market Return Assumption (MRA) is defined as
the one-year local market interest rate plus 5% (a proxy for, and not a forecast of, the equity risk premium). Under Review (UR)
Stocks may be flagged as UR by the analyst, indicating that the stock's price target and/or rating are subject to possible change in the

near term, usually in response to an event that may affect the investment case or valuation. Equity Price Targets have an investment
horizon of 12 months.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

considerations may be lower or higher than portfolios where ESG factors, exclusions, or other sustainability issues are not considered by the portfolio manager, and the
investment opportunities available to such portfolios may differ.

Quanta (2382.TW): AI servers ramp up to drive growth ahead; model 
transition and PC shipment slowdown could weigh on 4Q26; Neutral
19 October 2026 | 1:57AM HKT
Quanta Sep revenues declined by 23% MoM, which we attributed to AI servers model 
transition, while PC entering quarter-end at strong MoM of +62% MoM. Looking 
forward, we expect monthly revenues to resume sequential growth in Oct to Dec, 
riding on reducing impact of AI servers model transition as new racks ramping up, 
along with growing general servers on agentic AI, and ASP increase on better 
specification and high raw material costs. We expect 4Q26E revenues to decline by 
4% QoQ, considering the high base in 3Q26 and the gradual ramp up of new AI 
server racks. As we highlighted in Oct (Global Server TAM updates), we expect global 
AI server racks (powered by NVIDIA and AMD) shipment at 63k / 110k / 164k in 
2026E-28E along with the specification upgrade to drive the ASP; we raised leading 
US CSPs in aggregate capex by 4% / 17% / 22% in 2026E-28E to US$1,397bn by 
2028E, reflecting our positive view on AI spending. Maintain Neutral on fair 
valuation.

Earnings revision: We raise revenues by 1% / 23% / 30% in 2026E-28E to reflect the 
AI server racks shipment ramp up and higher ASP on better specification. Given the

much lower GM of AI server racks compared to Quanta’s other products, our changes in 
gross profits narrow to 2% / 5% / 5% in 2026E-28E. Our opex ratio remains largely 
unchanged in 2026E-27E, while further improves in 2028E reflecting the enhancing 
operation efficiency when revenues scale increases and harvest from previous R&D 
investments. We change our non-op income and tax rate estimates in 2026E-28E closer 
to historical trend. Our net income is thus changed by -2% / +0% / +14% in 2026E-28E.

Valuation: We continue to use a near-term P/E to derive our 12M TP . Our target P/E 
multiple is at 10.7x 2027E (no change), which is derived from the refreshed peers’ 
correlation between forward trading P/E and earnings growth and Quanta’s forward year 
earnings growth. With our updated earnings estimates our 12M TP is revised up to 
NT$300 (vs. NT$299 previously). Our target P/E is still between the company’s historical 
avg. and avg. -1stv. trading P/E. Maintain Neutral.

### Page 3

Price Target Risks and Methodology - Quanta 
We are Neutral rated on Quanta. Our 12-month target price of NT$300 is based on an 
10.7x 2027E P/E, which references PC/server peers’ EPS growth vs. P/E multiple 
correlation. We see EPS growth as a major factor for the stock’s performance. Key 
upside/downside risks include: 1) stronger-/weaker-than-expected PC market 
recovery; 2) faster-/slower-than-expected AI server ramp-up; and 3) 
stronger-/weaker-than-expected demand for general servers.

## Document 159: 5年消失25%岗位，照片冲洗员、文员等面临淘汰！高盛量化“新技术冲击就业”速度

Source PDF: all report pdf/5年消失25%岗位，照片冲洗员、文员等面临淘汰！高盛量化“新技术冲击就业”速度.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-23
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

AI 取代⼈类的焦虑暂时退潮，但⾼盛警告：平静之下暗流涌动。若 AI 最终带来 15% ⽣产率
提升，过渡期内最多 9% 的员⼯将被替代；历史上受技术冲击最深的 25 类职业，五年内就业
平均萎缩 25% 。更危险的是，⼀旦经济⾛弱，企业将加速⽤ AI 压缩⼈⼒成本，冲击烈度可
能超出预期。
摘要：
今年春天， AI 取代⼈类⼯作的恐慌情绪⼀度达到顶峰。但随着美国经济展现出超预期的韧性，这
股焦虑已明显降温 ——AI 相关裁员公告数量从⾼点⼤幅回落，媒体关于 AI 导致失业的报道也退潮
⾄年初⽔平。然⽽，⾼盛提醒市场：冷静不等于安全， AI 对劳动⼒市场的冲击远未结束，只是在
等待⼀个更合适的时机爆发。
据追⻛交易台，⾼盛全球投资研究在 10 ⽉ 22 ⽇发布的报告中给出结论：如果 AI 最终带来 15% 的
⽣产率提升，历史经验显⽰，过渡期内最多约 9% 的员⼯可能暂时离开现有岗位；在技术让劳动
岗位变得冗余的职业中，就业⼈数五年平均下降约 25% 。
当前影响仍可⻅，但范围较窄。⾼盛称，AI 暴露度最⾼⾏业过去三个⽉就业增⻓较⻓期趋势少

约 1 万⼈，不过这⼀缺⼝⼤体被 AI 相关建筑岗位招聘增加所抵消。服务业横截⾯数据开始显⽰压
⼒， AI 采⽤率与失业率的相关性已转正且具统计显著性， AI 采⽤率与就业增⻓的相关性略为负
值，但统计显著性不⾜。
市场关注点也已转向投资回报。⾼盛称，当前资本开⽀预期意味着， AI ⽤⼾每年需在 AI 应⽤上⽀
出约 1 万亿美元，才能让超⼤规模云⼚商和应⽤供应商实现市场似乎已计⼊的利润。对投资者来
说，关键变量在于 AI 节省劳动⼒成本的速度，以及这种节省是否以裁员、离职和岗位消失体现。
1% ⽣产率冲击：失业率短期上升 0.3 ⾄ 0.4 个百分点
⾼盛⽤三种经验⽅法衡量技术对劳动⼒的历史冲击。第⼀种是总量层⾯的技术冲击模型。结果
显⽰，技术创新带来的劳动⽣产率每提升 1% ，失业率峰值会暂时上升 0.3 ⾄ 0.4 个百分点，约两
年后影响消退。
失业率变化会低估实际岗位冲击。部分被替代员⼯可能较快找到新⼯作，部分员⼯可能退出劳
动⼒市场，这会压低失业率指标对真实冲击的反映。⾼盛进⼀步拆分岗位流量后发现，每 1% 技

术驱动的⽣产率提升，会带来 0.5 ⾄ 0.6 个百分点的累计总岗位流失率上升，同时拖累总招聘率约
0.2 个百分点。
⾏业层⾯的结果相近。⾼盛使⽤⾏业层⾯经利⽤率调整的全要素⽣产率冲击数据后发现，每 1%
⽣产率提升，同样对应失业率上升 0.3 ⾄ 0.4 个百分点，总岗位流失率累计上升约 0.6 个百分点，
岗位获得率下降约 0.2 个百分点。
在⾼盛基准情形下， AI 最终可能带来 15% 的经济整体⽣产率提升。按上述历史关系估算， AI 过
渡期内最多约 9% 的员⼯可能从当前岗位被替代，招聘放缓还可能对整体就业形成约 3% 的额外拖
累。
25 个职业样本：技术冲击后五年就业平均减少 25%
⾼盛选取 25 个最容易受到 AI 技术冲击的职业，这些职业包括照⽚冲印员、校对员、档案⽂员
等，属于技术对劳动需求影响最⼤的样本。⾼盛称，这类样本更接近 AI 可能影响最深岗位的上限
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

情形。
结果显⽰，这 25 个职业在技术冲击后五年就业⼈数平均下降约 25% 。其中照⽚冲印员降幅最
⼤，五年岗位减少接近 60% ，数码摄影和相关技术直接压缩传统冲印需求。
⾼盛样本中的 25 个受技术冲击职业包括：
这些职业的共同点是，技术直接压缩了原有⼈⼯流程。照⽚冲印受到数码摄影冲击，校对受到
⽂字处理和拼写语法检查⼯具冲击，档案⽂员受到电⼦⽂档系统冲击，抄表员受到智能远程表
具冲击，旅⾏代理受到线上订票渠道冲击，银⾏柜员受到 ATM 和⼿机银⾏分流。
岗位减少主要来⾃分离率上升，招聘放缓贡献较⼩
⾼盛最重要的发现之⼀是，技术冲击下的就业收缩更多来⾃岗位分离。岗位分离包括离职、被
辞退等⼈员离开原岗位的情形，招聘减少也有影响，但幅度较⼩。
在 25 个职业样本中，有 13 个职业的年度总招聘率下降，平均降幅为 0.7 个百分点。相⽐之下，有
19 个职业的年度总分离率上升，平均升幅达到 3.8 个百分点。
联合考察招聘率和分离率后，⾼盛发现， 25 个职业中有 17 个职业显⽰岗位⼈数减少更偏向由分

离率上升驱动。这与总量模型和⾏业模型⼀致：技术冲击通常先体现在实际岗位流失，再体现
在招聘放缓。
这对市场跟踪 AI 冲击具有直接意义。⾼盛认为， AI 相关裁员应成为观察 AI 就业替代的⾼频指标。
若未来 AI 带来的⽣产率提升兑现，单纯观察招聘计划可能反应偏慢，裁员公告、岗位分离和职业

### Page 3

层⾯⼈数变化更能提供实时信号。
周期决定替代速度：经济放缓会放⼤ AI 冲击
⾼盛同时强调， AI 对就业的影响尚处早期。美国只有 23% 的机构已将 AI 纳⼊部分常规⼯作流程，
⽽且这⼀采⽤率仍会⾼估当前⾃动化程度，因为多数机构尚未把 AI 嵌⼊所有可应⽤流程。⾼盛
称，⼤部分 AI 驱动的⽣产率收益仍在未来。
历史数据显⽰，⾃动化冲击带有明显周期性。经济健康时，⾃动化相关劳动⼒扰动通常较有
限；经济转弱时，企业更倾向通过⾃动化压缩劳动⼒成本，岗位流失速度会加快。
⾏业模型显⽰，当整体失业率上升时，每 1% 技术驱动⽣产率提升对失业率的影响会⽐基准估算
⾼出 0.1 ⾄ 0.2 个百分点。职业层⾯样本也显⽰，在技术冲击后五年内，整体失业率每额外上升 1
个百分点，受冲击职业就业⼈数会额外下降约 4% 。
⾼盛的结论是，若经济活动保持健康， AI 劳动⼒转型⼤概率可控，冲击将逐步显现；若经济进⼊
周期性放缓， AI 相关⾃动化和岗位流失可能加速，并放⼤商业周期波动。对投资者⽽⾔，未来
需要同时跟踪 AI 资本开⽀、企业劳动成本节省、 AI 相关裁员公告和宏观就业周期。

주: K-IFRS 연결 기준 
자료: HD현대중공업, LS증권 리서치센터

HiMSEN 엔진 및 SMR 주기기 생산설비 대규모 증설 발표 
- 9/10 HD현대중공업은 총 1조 722억원 규모의 시설투자 2건을 공시 
① 대출력 발전엔진(4행정 중속엔진, HiMSEN엔진) 생산능력 확충에 8,336
억원 투입, 글로벌 전력수요 증가에 따른 엔진발전 시장 확대에 대응하는 목
적. 대출력 발전엔진 전용 공장 신설과 토지 취득, 건축물·기계설비 등 생산설
비 구축이 골자. 투자기간은 ‘26.09~’28.05(설계/인허가 ~’27.02, 공장건설 
~’28.05. ’28.06 상업운전개시, 28년 가동률 30%, 2030년 100% 도달) 
② SMR 주기기 제작 생산시설 구축에 2,386억원(자기자본 대비 2.55%) 투
입, SMR 주기기 제작사업 진입 및 수주 물량 증가에 대응. SMR 전용 공장 
및 설비 신설과 기존 공장 최적화를 병행, 투자기간은 ’26.10~’29.04. 
기다리던 증설 공시. 늘어난 Capa로 AI DC향 수혜 확대 기대 
- 25년 3.0GW(선박용 2.3GW + 육상용 0.7GW)였던 HiMSEN 엔진 생산 
Capa는 2030년 7.2GW(선박용 3.2GW + 육상용 4.0GW)로 확대. 그간 선
박용 중심이었던 엔진 사업의 포트폴리오를 발전용으로 본격 다각화하는 사
업 재편으로 해석 
- AI DC 신규 증설 확대 추세 속 계통 연결의 어려움과 CCGT, Aero -
derivative 대비 납기 및 부하상승시간, 모듈화 용이성 영역에서 장점을 가진 
발전용 중속엔진에 대한 수요 지속 기대(260908 당사 보고서 참조) 
- 지난 Corban Energy Group 향 계약 건(1,000MW, $ 674 mn)을 단순 
적용 시 금번 증설의 향후 매출액 기여규모는 연간 $ 2.8 bn에 달함. 엔진 시
장의 Seller-Market 기조에 따라 20%를 상회하는 높은 이익률이 지속될 것
으로 전망함에 따라 향후 유의미한 이익 성장 기대 
- HD현대마린솔루션 또한 LTSA 수주 저변 확대 측면에서 향후 이익 확장 가
능. HiMSEN엔진 고유 라이선스 보유에 따른 시너지로 그룹사 전반의 수혜 
기대

표1 HD 현대중공업(329180): 신규시설투자 공시(2026.09.10) 
구분 ① 대출력 발전엔진 생산능력 확충 ② SMR 주기기 제작 생산시설 구축 
투자금액 8,336억원 2,386억원 
투자목적 글로벌 전력수요 증가에 따른 엔진발전 시장 확대 대응 SMR 주기기 제작사업 진입  및 수주 물량 증가 대응 
투자내용 대출력 발전엔진 전용 공장 신설, 토지 취득 및 건축물·기계설비 등 생산설비 구축 SMR 전용 공장 및 설비 구축, 기존 공장 최적화 
투자기간 2026.09.10 ~ 2028.05.31 2026.10.01 ~ 2029.04.30 
자료: DART, LS증권 리서치센터

2 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Consumers Feel One Way, but Spend Another
Kirsten Cabacungan, Vice President and Senior Investment Strategist
Americans have spent much of the past several years telling surveys they are cautious
about the economy. Yet they continue to spend as though they are not. The University
of Michigan’s Consumer Sentiment Index remains near multidecade lows, while the
Conference Board’s Index of Consumer Confidence has also been in a yearslong downtrend.

These surveys reflect a list of persistent concerns from elevated food and gas prices to
geopolitical uncertainty and labor market anxiety.
Even so, consumer spending has remained remarkably resilient. The final estimate of Q2
real consumer spending, as measured by PCE, was revised up to an annualized rate of
3.8%, well above the 2.3% 20-year average. More recent data suggest that momentum
has continued. According to Bank of America Institute, the latest monthly total credit and

debit card spending data per household rose 6.2% year-over-year (YoY) in September, and
spending excluding gasoline was up 5.1% YoY, both the third strongest growth rates in the
past four years. 1
The persistence of consumer spending is particularly notable given that temporary cyclical
boosts like larger tax refunds are starting to fade. That raises an important question: What is
actually funding strong consumption today? Recent data suggest the drivers are becoming

increasingly more diversified, with broadening labor market trends, healthy consumer
finances and elevated wealth effects all reinforcing one another and underpinning consumers.
First, the labor market continues to provide an important foundation for consumer spending,
and recent trends suggest wage growth is supporting a broader set of households than in
recent years. Jobs growth has slowed from the exceptionally strong pace seen following the
pandemic, but broader indicators suggest labor market conditions remain relatively stable.

Although the September jobs report came in softer than expected, the three- and six-month
rolling averages indicate that the trend in jobs growth likely bottomed out last year and has
gradually improved since (Exhibit 1A). Meanwhile, the unemployment rate remains low by
historical standards and layoffs remain contained. And while wage growth has moderated
and recently trailed headline inflation (Exhibit 1B), cumulative income gains over the past
several years have likely helped many households absorb higher prices better.

An important development though has been improving wage growth trends among lower-
income households, where spending tends to be most sensitive to changes in wages. Bank
of America Institute consumer deposit account data show after-tax wage growth for lower-
income households outpaced higher-income households for a second consecutive month
in August, rising 4.7% YoY compared with 3.5% for higher-income households. Importantly,
this improvement appears to be supporting discretionary spending, not just spending

on necessities. Lower-income discretionary spending rose 5.7% YoY in August, nearly
matching the 5.9% pace for higher-income households. In other words, the narrowing gap
in wage and spending growth across income cohorts suggests consumer resilience looks
to be becoming less dependent on affluent households alone, creating a broader and more
durable foundation for consumption.
Second, household finances remain in relatively good shape, providing consumers with the

flexibility to continue spending. The ratio of household debt to disposable personal income
remains near its lowest level since the late 1990s, excluding the temporary distortions
caused by pandemic-related income support in 2020 and 2021 (Exhibit 1C). Recent
revisions to the Bureau of Economic Analysis national income accounts also suggest
consumers have a larger financial buffer than previous data suggested. Personal saving
estimates over the past year have been revised higher by $320 billion, or 39%, and the

revised savings rate remains above 4% after previous estimates showed it fell to 3% during
the summer.2 Concerns that consumers may be rapidly exhausting their savings buffers
may therefore be overstated. Supporting this view, Bank of America Institute consumer
1 Bank of America Institute, “Consumer Checkpoint: Strength in numbers.” As of October 9, 2026.
2 Bank of America, “Massive Savings Markup = Another Reason to Be Bullish.” As of October 2, 2026.

Investment Implications
Consumer spending strength
remains a key pillar of economic
and earnings growth, which keeps
us constructive on our Equity
overweight. The growing connection
between household wealth and
spending reinforces the importance
of maintaining a diversified portfolio.

### Page 3

3 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
deposit account data show that middle- and lower-income households continue to maintain
deposit balances above prepandemic levels across age groups.
Third, elevated household wealth continues to provide a powerful tailwind for consumer
spending, particularly among households that account for an outsized share of overall
consumption. U.S. household net worth rose $12.8 trillion (T) to a record $195.9T in Q2,

driven largely by gains in Equities, which contributed $10.7T of the increase (Exhibit 1D).
As a result, household net worth relative to disposable income climbed to a record 8.3,
meaning households now hold more than eight dollars of wealth for every dollar of annual
disposable income. Households are also sitting on $35.8T in home equity, an all-time high
level that provides a significant balance sheet cushion. Rising asset values have helped to

support spending through what economists refer to as wealth effects. In other words, when
households feel wealthier, they tend to be more willing to spend.
For investors, the key takeaway is that consumer spending is being supported by multiple
engines despite the weaker sentiment data. Labor income trends are supporting wider
participation, healthy balance sheets are helping households absorb economic pressures,
and elevated household wealth continues to sustain higher-income spending. Together,

these forces are supportive of economic growth and corporate profits, which is particularly
important since the consumer accounts for roughly 70% of U.S. gross domestic product.
That said, it does not eliminate risk. A meaningful risk to the consumer would be a scenario
in which both the labor and wealth engines weaken simultaneously. For now, labor market
conditions remain stable, and wealth effects remain supportive. Equity market pullbacks in
recent years have done little to derail consumer spending, suggesting it would likely take a

deeper and more prolonged drawdown to alter consumer behavior.

MARKET VIEW
The Bull Market Turns Four: Older, Unloved—But Still Kicking
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
Four years ago this month, virtually no one was in the mood to buy U.S. Equities, and for
good reasons.
Rewind to October 2022: Inflation the month before clocked in at an annual rate of 8.2%.

The yield curve was inverted, a signal that has preceded most past recessions. Consumer
sentiment was abysmal, having dropped to a record low in June 2022. Recession
concerns were mounting, with an October Wall Street Journal poll survey putting the
odds of a recession within 12 months at about 63%. And realizing (belatedly) that
inflation was not “transitory,” the Fed was in the midst of administering one of the most
aggressive monetary tightening cycles in decades, raising the fed funds rate five times

between March and September 2022 by a stunning 300 basis points.
Reflecting the mood at the moment, the S&P 500 closed at 3,577 on October 12, 2022,
down 25% below its January peak, leaving investors battered and bruised but blind to the
fact that the market had bottomed. Four years on, the bull market that emerged from
the gloom of 2022 is still standing and kicking, with the S&P 500 up roughly 130% since
the October 2022 lows.
The more than doubling of the S&P over the past four years has hardly been linear—and

has been accompanied with plenty of pain and doubt. Exhibit 2 maps the rocky path
trodden by the bull over the past four years. Less than six months into the rally, the
markets were rocked by the failure of Silicon Valley Bank (SVB) on March 10, 2023. The
S&P Regional Banks Select Industry Index lost 18% that week and stoked concerns of
massive deposit runs and credit stress across the capital markets. However, owing to
emergency intervention by the government, lower yields and strength elsewhere in the

markets, the crisis passed. Some banks failed that month, but the bull market didn’t.
Exhibit 2: Revisiting the Rocky Path Trodden by the Bull.
Period "Reason to Sell" What happened instead
October 2022 Inflation >8%, aggressive Fed tightening, recession concerns Bull market begins
March 2023 SVB/regional banking crisis Stocks keep climbing
2023-24 Inverted yield curve, “recession inevitable” Economy keeps expanding
2024 “Magnificent 7* bubble,” narrow breadth, expensive valuations Earnings propel market higher

2025 Tariffs, trade war, policy uncertainty Market absorbs another shock
2026 Oil/diesel shock, 5%+ yields, Fed tightening, geopolitical risks Bull reaches fourth birthday
*Apple, Amazon, Alphabet, Nvidia, Meta, Microsoft and Tesla. Source: Chief Investment Office. As of October 9, 2026.
But recessionary concerns hung like threatening clouds over the markets for most of
2023. Aggressive Fed tightening, an inverted yield curve, and soft economic data were

all taken as harbingers of an economic slowdown and profits recession. Yet the S&P 500
continued to climb in 2023, up 26.3% for the year, driven in large part by the outsized
price performance of the Magnificent Seven.
Investors spent much of 2024 fretting about a market too dependent on technology,
and therefore a market too narrow and too expensive. The unwinding of the Japan carry
trade in August 2024 engendered more angst, as did uncertainties around the 2024

U.S. presidential election. But despite a host of misgivings, the S&P 500 delivered total
returns of 25% in 2024. The unloved bull marched on.
Total returns in 2025 were robust as well—roughly 18%—notwithstanding—yet
again—a number of headwinds. In late January, DeepSeek, a cheaper China AI model,
raised questions about America’s technology leadership and the need to spend enormous
sums on chips and data centers. The Nasdaq promptly dropped by 3.1% thereafter.

Investment Implications
The adoption phase of the AI buildout
should translate to broader earnings
growth and performance across
sectors, reinforcing our conviction
in U.S. Equities. Given a strong
fundamental backdrop, we would
continue to buy on weakness as the
bull enters its fifth year

### Page 5

5 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
January’s jolt was followed by “Liberation Day,” or the warning of punishing U.S. tariffs
on America’s major trading partners. The trade-policy shock of April 2025 caused the
S&P 500 to drop nearly 19% from its February high to its April 8 low on uncertainty
that higher import costs would decrease corporate margins and consumer spending and
upend global supply chains. Amid the trade tumult, Treasury prices fell and the U.S. dollar

weakened on concerns that foreigners were abandoning U.S. assets. Remember all the
angst over the dollar debasement trade? If that wasn’t enough, expensive valuations and
enormous capital commitments raised doubts about the AI trade and triggered talk of an
AI bubble. Yet despite this wall of worry, the S&P 500, for the third year running, posted
double-digit gains in 2025.
And double-digit gains could be in store this year, with the S&P 500 up 14% through

October 9. The bull has rumbled on, taking in stride the energy shock from the war in
the Middle East, the shift in interest rate expectations from the Fed (from rate cuts
to rate hikes), and the rising uncertainty associated with the midterm elections. These
headwinds have been countered by tailwinds such as better-than-expected corporate
earnings and better-than-anticipated real growth.
Finally, as Exhibit 3 makes clear, what has given this bull so much muscle has been

the U.S. Technology sector, specifically the Magnificent 7. Powering the bull has been
the Magnificent 7, with a staggering 344% return since the 2022 lows, while U.S.
technology has delivered a 277% return versus the S&P 500’s return of 130%. Note the
outperformance of the S&P 500 relative to the main international indices.
Looking forward: Is the bull’s greatest strength also its Achilles’ heel? Bull
markets don’t die of natural causes. And they typically don’t expire because of one

particular signal or indicator. Bull markets turn to bear markets when a cluster of signals/
metrics turn south. Think deteriorating and downward earnings revisions, higher interest
rates, widening credit spreads and tighter lending standards, growth disappointments,
stretched valuations and sentiment, deteriorating market breadth, and an external
shock. Any one of these signals could flash red and not trouble the bull.  But when they
converge or feed off each other, that’s trouble.

In addition, since a great deal of the earnings growth, market leadership and capital
expenditures (capex) spending of this bull market have come from the technology sector,
the bull’s greatest strength could become its greatest vulnerability. AI has become an
investment ecosystem unto itself, and hence the fifth year of the bull market will pivot
in part around AI revenue, frontier model adoption rates, infrastructure spending, free
cash flows, depreciation costs, debt issuance, data center utilization, cloud growth, and

competition via cheaper models and computing alternatives. To put it mildly, there is a
great deal at stake when it comes to the bull and AI.
However, this is where we stand today: We believe the bull will continue to rumble
ahead next year, with AI revenues and productivity gains justifying the costs of the AI
infrastructure. Critically, AI is moving from the buildout phase, dominated by chipmakers
and hyperscaler capex, into the adoption phase, where software firms, healthcare,

finance, energy and a myriad of other firms start to see productivity gains and margin
expansion from rising AI adoption rates. We believe this will give better breadth to the
S&P 500; so too will continued profit growth across various U.S. sectors, AI investment
that starts to pay off, and an economy capable of absorbing setbacks or periodic bouts
of uncertainty.
The path forward won’t be linear—and there will be fits and starts, and market
drawdowns. That’s normal for any market. But that aside, the bull will keep rumbling,

roaming and kicking in 2027.

### Page 6

6 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THE TICKER TAPE
What is our view of the macro and market landscape heading
into 2027 and beyond?
Christopher Hyzy, Chief Investment Office
A supply side-led economic expansion continues, driven by capital investment in the AI
buildout, solid consumer spending and fiscal tailwinds still blowing, albeit with less force.
This creates a backdrop that keeps nominal growth well-above trend and, importantly,

above the interest cost on national debt.
The interest rate backdrop largely remains the same as of today, but, instead of the
sharp increases that we experienced in 2026, the curve should settle down, and yields
should remain relatively range bound. The Fed is likely to tap on the breaks a couple of
more times before reassessing new inflation, growth and employment trends later in
2027.
Concerns surrounding credit risk and "circular" financing are likely to rise the further we

move into the AI buildout, but we do not expect a default cycle to develop. Rather, we
expect the sharp backup in yields to place pressure namely on lower-quality credits in
areas having a difficult time building the cash flow necessary to pay the higher interest
costs.
We expect a broadening out within the market but technology to still maintain its
heavyweight positioning. As we move from training and evolving the large language
models to agentic, and physical AI new leaders are likely to develop. In addition, we

anticipate AI adopters such as biotechnology, transport, logistics, advance manufacturing
and financial companies to enter new growth and productivity phases.
Lastly, overall earnings growth in the U.S. should be low to mid double-digit percent
stages in 2027, which is about half of the growth from 2026 thus far but still around
double from normal levels.
Credit risks developing and higher energy prices for longer are the main risks to our bull
case for Equities extending further into 2027 and beyond.

Index Definitions
ecurities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

University of Michigan’s Consumer Sentiment Index is a monthly survey of consumer confidence levels in the United States conducted by the University of Michigan.
Conference Board’s Index of Consumer Confidence measures what consumers are feeling about their expected financial situation, whether that's optimistic or pessimistic.
Consumer Price Index is a statistical measure that tracks the average change over time in the prices paid by urban consumers for a representative "market basket" of everyday goods and

services.
Nasdaq Composite Index is a stock market measure that tracks the performance of more than 3,000 stocks listed on the Nasdaq exchange.
MSCI Emerging Markets c is a stock market index that tracks the performance of large-cap and mid-cap companies across 24 developing and fast-growing nations.
MSCI All-Country World Index ex USA is a market-capitalization-weighted stock market index that tracks large- and mid-cap equity performance across developed and emerging markets

worldwide, excluding the United States.

## Document 257: Cliff Notes_ mulling the minutes (2026-10-24)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Cliff Notes_ mulling the minutes (2026-10-24).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-24
Pages: 11
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: mulling the minutes
By Sian Fenner, Ryan Wells & Illiana Jain
11:10 October 09 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
The only data release of note in Australia this week was the October Westpac-MI Consumer Sentiment
Survey, which reported a 4.7% drop in the headline index. At 80.4, sentiment has tumbled back to
deeply pessimistic levels, similar to the post-pandemic cost-of-living shock when readings were among

the weakest in the survey’s more than fifty-year history. Indeed, inflation and interest rate pressures are
once again clearly front of mind for consumers, with both the view on ‘family finances vs a year ago’

### Page 2

and ‘family finance next 12 months’ falling 8.0% and 6.4% in the month, respectively. Moreover, the
RBA’s September rate hike looks to have had a significant impact – sentiment tanked 20% between
those surveyed before the RBA decision and those surveyed afterwards.

Views on the economic outlook were relatively more resilient, but they still weakened, with the one-year
and five-year ahead sub-indexes falling 1.6% and 0.4% respectively. Consumers are becoming more
‘on edge’ about job security, albeit not outright alarmed. The unemployment rate remains relatively low
versus history, although it has drifted up to a cycle high of 4.6% as labour supply continues to outstrip
demand. We expect it to hold there in next week’s September Labour Force Survey – see our preview
for more detail. All told, with higher interest rates, renewed inflation pressures and a softening jobs
market all weighing at once, households are entering the final months of the year on the back foot.

Offshore, the focus was on US data.

Before turning to this week's releases, it is worth noting that September non-farm payrolls came in
softer than expected across most metrics. Employment rose by only 29k (89k expected), and there
were sizeable downward revisions to prior months, with a combined 60k removed from July and August
payrolls, leaving gains of just 31k and 29k respectively. The details pointed to weaker hiring in leisure &
hospitality and education & health services industries, although construction and manufacturing
employment continued to benefit from ongoing data-centre-related investment. The unemployment rate
edged up to 4.2%, with softer employment occurring alongside a rise in labour force participation to
61.8%. Meanwhile wage growth moderated to 0.1%mth (3.0%yr). Taken together, the report suggests
labour demand is cooling, with the three-month average pace of job creation slowing to around 50k.
However, softer payroll gains are not necessarily indicative of labour market deterioration. Lower
immigration and ageing demographics have reduced the pace of employment growth needed to keep
the unemployment rate stable. As such, the report is more consistent with a labour market that is
gradually normalising than one entering a meaningful downturn.

The September FOMC minutes reinforced a distinctly hawkish message, with participants noting they
had "not seen sufficient progress on lowering inflation in recent months" and expressing concern that
inflation risks remained "skewed to the upside". Policymakers highlighted that both core services and
core goods inflation remained elevated, with some attributing persistent goods price strength to the
effects of the AI buildout despite the sector's traditional disinflationary role. There was also concern
that, after more than five years of inflation above target, elevated inflation could begin to influence
inflation expectations and wage- and price-setting behaviour. In contrast, labour market conditions
were generally viewed as stable and "close to maximum employment". While a majority noted the
labour market had strengthened somewhat recently, several participants highlighted unusually low
dynamism, characterised by low hiring, low layoffs, a low job-finding rate and elevated long-term
unemployment. Importantly, participants generally judged that aggregate wage growth was moderate
and "consistent with inflation moving toward 2 percent", with several explicitly noting that "the labor
market was not currently a source of inflationary pressures".

### Page 3

Related articles
A key focus was the interaction between AI investment, productive capacity and inflation. While
participants broadly expected AI-related investment to boost productivity growth and potential output
over time, several warned that "the AI buildout could cause aggregate demand to outpace aggregate
supply over the medium term, putting upward pressure on inflation". Consistent with this view, some
participants had raised their estimate of the neutral policy rate and several judged the current policy
stance to be "not restrictive or only mildly restrictive". Against this backdrop, all participants supported
the September rate increase, and most assessed that another increase in the federal funds rate would
likely be appropriate by year-end, although future decisions would remain data dependent.

The September ISM Services PMI released this week painted a similar picture of an economy that
remains resilient but is gradually losing momentum. The headline index eased to 54.9 from 55.4 in
August, with the decline driven by a sharp fall in business activity and a 9.4pt drop in new export
orders, which slipped into contractionary territory for the first time in eight months. Labour market
conditions improved modestly with the employment index moving to broadly neutral territory 50.1 after
two months below 50, supported by elevated backlogs and still-solid demand. Respondent
commentary illustrated the mixed nature of labour demand, with some firms reporting they were "filling
positions vacated due to promotions or retirements", while others cited "restructuring due to efficiencies
gained using AI tools". Cost pressures also remained elevated, with the prices paid index rising to 74.0,
its highest level since July 2022 and around 18pts above its historical average, while fuel costs, tariffs
and supply chain constraints remained the most frequently cited concerns. Overall, the report suggests
services activity remains consistent with ongoing expansion, albeit at a slower pace, while labour
demand and pricing pressures remain firm.

Overall, the data supports our view that the FOMC will move to a broadly neutral policy stance by the
end of the year with one more rate hike, although the case for a back-to-back move in October has
become less clear cut.

## Document 287: Fed builds credibility with hike; one more likely in Dec

Source PDF: all report pdf/Fed builds credibility with hike; one more likely in Dec.pdf
Report source: 渣打銀行 (Market Watch)
Date: 2026-09-17
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Fed builds credibility with 
hike; one more likely in Dec 
Summary 
Event: The US Federal Reserve hiked its key interest rate by 
25bps to 3.75-4.0%, in a unanimous decision. This marks the 
first hike since 2023, reversing the easing cycle that had 
taken rates down from 4.25% in September 2025 to a trough 
of 3.75% by December 2025. The revised projections 
indicate the Fed expects to raise rates by an additional 25bps 
this year, hold rates in 2027, and cut 25bps in both 2028 and 
2029. It upgraded the long-run rate slightly to 3.2%.  
Market reaction: The US yield curve mildly flattened , while 
the S&P500 index fell 0.5%. The policy rate sensitive US 2 -
year government bond yield jumped 7 bps to 4. 74% as 
markets factored in  three more 25bps hikes by June 2027 . 
The US 10-year yield rose 2bps to 5.02%, its highest since 
2007, while the 30-year yield was little changed. The USD 
index (DXY) rose 0.6%, breaking past its 50- and 100-DMAs. 
Investment strategy: Today’s move realises what we had 
been flagging in recent weeks i.e. the rising risk of a Fed rate 
hike following Fed Chair Warsh’s Jackson Hole speech and 
recent economic data prints, namely: a pick-up in job creation 
and inflation over the summer and a sharp rebound in oil 
prices due to the re-escalation of the Middle East conflict.  
This hike helps the Fed rebuild credibility as an inflation 
fighter. Given the unanimous decision to hike rates this week, 
we believe there will likely be one more hike before the end 
of this year, and one more in H1 2027 as we expect robust 
growth amid strong AI investments and a tight labour market, 
keeping inflation elevated.  That scenario would still be less 
than the three more rate hikes money markets are pricing 
after the latest Fed move. We believe policy rates can return 
to the 4.0 –4.25% range by end-2027, with the possibility of 
cuts in H2 2027  as the impact of tariffs and high oil prices 
start to fade towards the second half of 2027.   
Today’s refreshed Fed policy rate path is mildly bullish for the 
USD index; so we revise up our 3 -month target to 100. We 
also revise up our 3-month 10-year yield target to 5.0-5.25%.  
 The USD rose, while stocks fell after the Fed hiked rates 
and signalled one more rate hike this year; ultra-long 
bonds rose slightly as the Fed rebuild its inflation-
fighting credentials

still sees inflation cooling over the next two years

### Page 2

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 17 September 2026

Backdrop to the rate hike 
In hiking rates, Fed Chair Warsh reaffirmed the inflation 
threat to the US economy. US headline inflation accelerated 
to 0.4% m/m in August and stayed elevated on an annualised 
basis at 3.4% y/y. Energy prices , driven by the Middle East 
conflict, were a primary contributor. Oil prices have extended 
gains since the August inflation report, surging past USD 
100/bbl. Meanwhile, core inflation rose 0.3% m/m in August, 
beating estimates for a 0.2% rise . Also, US producer prices 
jumped 0.4% m/m in August, the most since May, and 5.4%  
y/y, further reinforcing the inflationary backdrop. 
The inflation report follow ed a stronger -than-expected jobs 
report for August. US monthly job creation has surged to an 
average 80,000 this year, from 10,000, tightening a job 
market that already faces a supply crunch from the Trump 
administration’s clampdown against immigration.  The 
combination of an acceleration in job creation and inflation 
over the summer, combined with a rebound in oil prices, 
supported our expectation for a Fed rate hike this week.  
Removing “a dose of accommodation” 
Warsh framed the decision to hike as removing "a dose of 
accommodation", rather than the start of an aggressive rate 
hiking cycle, and we agree with that characterisation. The aim 
is to bring financial and credit conditions more in line with the 
Fed's price stability objectives. To quote Warsh: “Our 
predominant focus is on the price stability side of our 
mandate. The plain fact is that inflation is too high, and has 
been for too long….This  summer’s inflation readings do not 
tell me that underlying trends have meaningfully improved.” 
He added that many categories in the last consumer and 
producer inflation reports are still showing price increases 
above 3% on a six- and 12-month basis.  
The unanimous vote to hike rates, contrasts with a 9 -3 split 
vote in July to hold rates. When combined with the pencilling 
in of one more hike by December, this suggests a majority of 
Fed policymakers have turned more hawkish over the 
summer. In doing so, they have helped rebuild the Fed’s 
inflation-fighting credentials and its independence against 
President Trump’s pressures to cut rates. This explains why 
long-end bond yields were stable after this week’s hike. 
Nevertheless, Warsh provided little guidance on the future 
rate path, as he held what was reportedly the shortest post -
meeting press conference on record for a Fed chair . This is 
consistent with his stated preference for less central bank 
communication. We expect more clarity once the Fed task 
forces submit their reports by year end. 
— Jonathan Liang, CIO for Fixed Income and FX   
Rajat Bhattacharya, Senior Investment Strategist

官員們認為年底前至少還有一次升息 ，主因
通膨偏高 、經濟與勞動市場保持韌性 ，因此

升息可能仍有必要。
► 日股表現受惠大型科技 、半導體設備及 AI概
念股，且日本央行雖升息但並無更鷹派的態
度，日圓走弱並激勵出口類股走勢 。歐股則
缺乏明確方向 ，雖科技股彈升及油價回落提
供支撐 ，然而全球央行緊縮及公債殖利率走
升壓抑估值 ，且法國財政疑慮亦推升風險溢
價，德國因執政聯盟主要政黨在地方選舉遭
遇挫敗，增加了政策與政治上的不確定性。
► 產業部分 ， 除了有 AI 題材帶動的科技及通
訊，工業類股亦有不錯表現 ，其餘類股則漲
跌互見。

► 隨人工智慧運算需求持續攀升 ，谷歌、亞馬
遜、臉書、微軟及OpenAI等企業巨頭積極發
展客製化晶片 (ASIC)，藉此提升運算效率並
優化成 本 。根據 凱 基投顧 引述產 業數 據預
估，2027年客製化晶片出貨量將年增 92%至
1,446萬顆 ，首度超 越繪圖處理器 (GPU)的
1,129萬顆 ，顯示AI晶片市場正由 GPU主導
逐步轉向多元化發展。
► 在客製化晶片方面 ，谷歌持續擴大自研張量
處理器 (TPU)的應用與對外供應 ，亞馬遜亦
積極拓展自研AI晶片(Trainium)的雲端運算服
務，預估兩者合計將佔 2027年客製化晶片出
貨量約80%。
► 隨客製 化晶 片需 求 快速增 加 ，相 關晶 片設
計、先 進製 程及 先 進封裝 需求可 望同 步提
升，涉及上述業務發展的台股供應鏈 (註)有機
會受到市場關注。

### Page 6

6
投資焦點
► 2024年全球工廠運作的工業機器人約 470萬
台，為10年前的 3倍，主要為韓國 、中國和
日本等製造業大國將其運用於汽車 、電子和
金屬加工等產業 ，並因而維持成本和品質優
勢，為競爭力重要來源之一 。雖然美國工業
機器人的普及程度仍低於亞洲主要製造業國
家，但近年積極尋求重建工業產能 、將關鍵
供應鏈遷回美國國內以及擴大半導體和製藥
生產，有可能讓美國逐步調整工業和製造層
面的策略方向。
► 傳統工業機器人與人形機器人在外觀設計與
應用場景上有明顯差異 。工業機器人通常部
署於高度標準化的環境 ，以執行範圍相對有
限且重複性高的特定作業為主 ，而人形機器
人則被設計用於多變的環境 ，並經常需要與
人類協同工作 ，因此需結合移動能力 、感知
能力與靈巧操作能力 ，才能夠執行多樣化任
務，而非受限單一功能。
工業機器人技術相對成熟且部署速度較快全球運作中的工業機器人數量穩定成長
工業機器人技術成熟且穩定成長，為製造業強國的重要競爭力來源

### Page 7

7
投資焦點人形機器人
► 人 形機 器 人 主 要由 結 合半 導 體 與AI 模 型的
「大腦」，並由感測器 、執行器 、線材、連
接器及輕量化電池驅動的機身所組成 ，可透
過感知環境資訊 、思考並進行協調的實體動
作。投行預估至2030年，執行器將佔整體人
形機器人成本超過 50%，為價值含量最高的
核心零組件 ，麥肯錫公司甚至認為執行器與
感測器可能成為未來供應鏈中的關鍵瓶頸。
► 機器人在實體世界中執行任務時 ，任何延遲
或連線中斷都可能帶來重大風險 ，因此相較
純數位的 AI應用 ，對即時運算 (邊緣運算 )能
力的要求更高 。而近幾年邊緣運算軟硬體能
力大幅提升 ，使本地端設備得以部署數十億
參數的 AI模型 ，並將反應延遲縮短至約 0.3
秒，使機器人能夠即時感知 、決策與行動 。
此外，過去6年風險資本對機器人、感測器及
IT 硬體的每年平均投資約 100 億美元 ， 但
2026年僅上半年即已達 280億美元 ，暗示市
場對機器人產業潛力的期待有別於過往。
機器人技術進展快速並開始吸引風險資本投資人形機器人運作有賴AI模型與大量機身零組件整合

人形機器人產業結合AI與硬體，含金量高吸引風險資本進駐

### Page 8

8
股市
◆ 美、歐、日央行升息抑通膨 ，公債殖利率走高壓抑股債市場近期動能 。然而包括油價自高檔回落使
通膨壓力稍緩 ，以及美股第三季獲利年增率仍為雙位數成長 ，股市表現有支撐 。市場持續觀察AI資
本支出是否帶來獲利成長 ，短期內評價面壓力將增添市場動盪 ，可分散配置於不同國家與產業 ，降
低市場波動影響 。總結上述，地緣衝突、殖利率偏高及評價面壓力等紛擾帶來負面影響 ，可布局防
禦股抗震，長期而言，科技及AI半導體發展趨勢不變且基本面佳，可逢拉回時分批布局。
◆ 歐、日股可作為分散配置重點 ，加上全球利率走揚有利銀行股 。此外，地緣衝突頻傳 ，歐洲國防軍
工仍有投資題材。
策略風格：短期可增加防禦股配置，中長
期看好AI題材如科技及半導體，非AI部分
可關注金融產業
區域：日本銀行股、歐洲金融、歐洲國防
債市
◆ 全球公債殖利率攀升 ，且最新聯準會利率點陣圖顯示 ，官員預估今年底前仍有一碼升息空間 ，立場
偏向維持較高利率水平抑制通膨發展 ，加上9月製造業及服務業PMI優於預期，殖利率偏高環境建議

以中短天期債券投資為主 ，可逢利率走升時布局進行鎖利領息 。高信評債體質較佳 ，具穩定現金流
與還款能力，聚焦風險調整後利差具吸引力者，包含金融、科技、通訊及公用事業等產業。
◆ 可以非美貨幣債券如歐元或澳幣計價投資級債券，分散單一貨幣風險。
債種：中短天期高信評債鎖利，產業如金
融、科技、通訊及公用事業等
衛星配置：非美貨幣債券分散風險
匯市
◆ 聯準會升息且態度偏鷹，美元高檔盤整。
◆ 日本銀行9月升息，但前瞻指引偏中性，未如市場預期的鷹派，日圓震盪。
美元高檔整理，日圓震盪
商品
◆ 中東地緣衝突加劇推升油價，短期紛擾難解，偏高的油價可能對通膨及實體經濟帶來影響。
◆ 短期受美國雙率走高有壓 ，惟美日財政赤字壓力與去美元化使全球央行買盤依舊 ，提供黃金中長期
穩定支撐。

### Page 15

個別客戶因年齡、投資能力、金融商品交易經驗及風險承受度不同，適合之服務及/或金融商品亦有不同，應進一步與專屬理財業務人員進行評估。投資人應徹底瞭解相關服務及金融商品之風險 ，並審慎評估個人財力、資產狀況、
理財需求及風險承受能力，獨立判斷並自行決定是否投資及承擔有關風險。投資任何商品皆具有風險，商品以往之績效不保證未來之投資收益，本公司不負責商品之盈虧，也不保證最低之收益。
本公司保留對本文件及其內容之著作權及其他相關權利，任何人未經本公司同意，不得翻印或作其他任何用途使用(包括但不限於傳送或轉發予其他人士等)。如有違反情事，本公司除不負任何責任外，亦將對未經同意之使用與散布
等行為究責。
基金警語
投資一定有風險，基金投資有賺有賠，申購前應詳閱公開說明書
【信託產品投資須知及風險預告：（請投資人審慎評估下列各項條文內容）】
1. 基金（包括具有定期配息之基金）投資具投資風險，此一風險可能使信託本金發生全部虧損。
2. 基金委託人須自負盈虧，本公司受託投資不保本不保息，除盡善良管理人注意義務外，投資所可能產生的本金虧損、匯率損失、或基金解散、清算、移轉、合併等風險，均由委託人承擔。

3. 本公司受託投資之基金經金管會核准或同意生效，惟不表示絕無風險。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良管理人之注意義務外，不負責基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，委託人
申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
4. 投資前應詳閱風險預告及各該基金公開說明書 ，確實作好財務規劃與風險評估 。本公司受託投資之基金已備有公開說明書 (或其中譯本)或投資人須知【前述文件併揭露有關基金應負擔之費用 (境外基金含分銷費用)】，委託人可
至本公司營業處所索取或至公開資訊觀測站、基金資訊觀測站或本公司網站下載。
5. 為保護既有投資人之權益，部份境外基金設有價格稀釋調整機制及公平市價規定，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書及投資人須知。
6. 委託人瞭解並同意受託人辦理特定金錢信託業務(即委託人委託投資基金)交易時，自交易相對人取得之報酬、費用、折讓等各項利益，得作為受託人收取之信託報酬；另委託人在持有基金期間 ，受託人仍持續收受經理費分成報
酬，委託人可至受託人(即本公司)網站 (https://www.kgi.com.tw/zh-tw/)查詢基金之通路報酬。其基金經理費率及其分成費率與其他通路報酬最新變動情形，亦可至該網站查詢。

7. 後收型基金在贖回時，基金公司將依持有期間長短收取不同比率之遞延申購手續費 ，該費用將自贖回總額中扣除；另後收型基金若有收取分銷費 ，則申購手續費雖可遞延收取，惟每年仍需支付0%~1.06%的分銷費（按基金日平
均淨資產價值計算），可能造成實際負擔費用增加（分銷費依各基金公司而有不同，請參考本公司後收型基金投資費用暨特約事項及各基金之投資人須知），另該費用已反映於每日基金淨值中，委託人無需額外支付。
8. 委託人之交易如經基金公司認定為短線交易者，基金公司或本公司可拒絕受理申請轉換或申購要求，並可請求支付一定比例之贖回費用或其他相關費用，費用標準均依各該基金公司之規定。
9. 委託人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績效亦不代表未來績效之保證。基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動，投資人於獲配息時，宜一併注意基
金淨值之變動。又投資新興市場可能比投資已開發國家有較大的價格波動及流動性較低的風險，委託人應慎選投資標的。
10. 部分基金可能持有衍生性商品部位，該部位可能超過基金淨資產價值之100%，並可能因而造成基金淨值高度波動及衍生其他風險。

11. 基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部分，可能導致原始投資金額減損；且部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之費用。由本金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至證券投資信託事業或總代理人之公司
網站查詢。
12. 非投資等級債券基金（本類基金主要係投資於非投資等級之高風險債券且配息來源可能為本金）（主要特色係投資前揭基金之組合基金亦同），適合能承受較高風險之『非保守型』委託人且不宜占投資組合過高的比重。由於非
投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損，不適合無法承擔相關風險之委託人；又如
係本國投信機構募集者，則可能投資於符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，雖其投資總金額不得超過基金淨資產價值之百分之三十，然該債券具有債券發行人違約之信用風險、利率風險、流動性風險、交易對手風險
及因財務訊息揭露不完整或因價格不透明導致波動性較大之風險，投資人應審慎評估；另證券投資信託事業募集之債券型基金、平衡型基金、不動產證券化基金、以追蹤、模擬或複製標的指數表現之指數股票型基金（ETF）及

指數型基金或募集之基金投資新興市場國家債券達基金淨資產價值之百分之六十以上者，或有可能投資符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，亦應留意相關風險。
13. 投資於中國之基金應遵守中華民國相關法令之規定：境外基金 (不含經金管會核准向證交所申請上市或交易之境外 ETF)如有投資大陸地區之有價證券者，以掛牌上市有價證券及銀行間債券市場為限 ，且投資前述有價證券總金額
不得超過該境外基金淨資產價值之百分之二十(境外基金機構依鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫向金管會申請並經認可者得適用鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫所列放寬前揭投資總金額上限至百分之四十之優惠措施 )；證券投資信託基金雖可投資
大陸地區之有價證券，惟仍應符合信託契約、公開說明書、證券投資信託基金管理辦法等相關規定及受到基金公司QFII額度之限制。又投資人須留意中國市場之政府政策、法令、會計稅務制度、經濟與市場等變動所可能產生之
投資風險。
14. 各基金於公開（含簡式）說明書或投資人須知揭露之風險報酬等級，係依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」而訂定，本公司亦依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」訂定基金產品風險等級。

富管理客服專線或透過財富管理客服信箱服務，查詢申訴案件處理進度。

United States: Elevated inflation and high growth
02/10/2026 4
August 2026: Marked improvement in services
business confidence, from an already high level
September 2026: Manufacturing business

confidence remains stable at a high level
August 2026: Increasing inflationary pressures September 2026: Supplier deliveries do not get
longer
August 2026: Consumers are a bit more
concerned about their personal finances
August 2026: Headline inflation resists to the
upside
U.S. macroeconomic performance has remained solid since the onset of the shock, as illustrated in particular by Q2 growth
of +3.8% and +9% (annualized quarterly rate) in consumer spending and private nonresidential investment, respectively.

In the manufacturing sector, business sentiment has gained momentum despite the shock, while the impact of this shock
on the services sector has remained very limited. The immediate reaction of consumer confidence was, however, more
negative before beginning to improve, which was nevertheless interrupted in August (latest available data for households’
sentiment on their future personal financial situation). The University of Michigan’s composite indicator continued to

deteriorate in September, due to the sharp rise in fuel prices. But declining confidence still does not translate into reduced
spending—at least not at the aggregate level.
On the inflation front, the spike immediately following the outbreak of the conflict in Iran was more pronounced than in
the months following Russia’s invasion of Ukraine. The decline in June still left inflation at a high level, but its stability in
July and August was notable (3.4% y/y), as was the latest—the third consecutive—slight decline in inflation excluding

energy in August (2.5% y/y, same rate as in February). Given the existing inflationary pressures, however, inflation is highly
likely to resume its upward trend in September.
Source: BLS, ISM, NFIB, University of Michigan, BNP Paribas

## Document 404: ‘Cost-Plus’ Costs (2026-10-24)

Source PDF: all report pdf/‘Cost-Plus’ Costs (2026-10-24).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-24
Pages: 11
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#australia #luciellis #labour #rba #productivity
‘Cost-Plus’ Costs
By Luci Ellis
Chief Economist Westpac Group, Westpac
10:40 October 16 2026 Share
Entrenched expectations that cost increases can and should be passed on are precisely
what tight monetary policy is leaning against, but only indirectly.
The recent tendency of some firms to bundle into prices card-acceptance costs that had previously
been surcharged – and then some, in some cases – demonstrates a ‘cost-plus’ approach to pricing.

### Page 2

The recent banning of card surcharges has caused considerable upset this month, even though it is a
reversion to historical and international practice. The card schemes used to impose “no-surcharge”
rules, until the RBA banned these in 2003, and many other countries still have them. The turnaround
represents the RBA throwing in the towel on its longstanding effort to use price incentives to get people
to choose low-cost payment methods. Surcharges no longer made sense in a largely cashless
economy. In any case, least-cost routing puts the power to choose lower-cost methods in the hands of
the merchants who actually bear the cost.

In most cases, firms will simply roll the surcharge into the sticker price, leaving the total cost to the
consumer unchanged, just more transparent. We also understand that some large retailers have
absorbed the cost – if they were surcharging in the first place, which many did not. (The RBA estimated
that around 16% of businesses imposed surcharges.) Inevitably, though, some firms have lifted their
prices by more than any reasonable estimate of the merchant service fee that the surcharge was
meant to cover. And because this is Australia, of course takeaway coffee has been the centre of
consumer discontent.

Higher prices for one’s caffeine fix was unlikely to be the main instigator of this month’s slump in
consumer sentiment. Petrol prices are typically more salient for cost-of-living concerns, and the before-
and-after picture on the RBA announcement that week shows that higher interest rates also contributed
to the decline.

In some senses, though, the post-surcharge prices and the RBA’s decisions are linked: businesses
passing on increased costs into their own prices – and expecting to be able to make that increase stick
– is exactly the kind of inflationary environment the RBA is leaning against. Firms have been subjected
to an array of non-labour cost escalations, from pandemic-era supply chain issues to electricity to fuel
to water to building supplies. And IT equipment prices, which for decades were a reliable source of cost
reductions, are nowadays flat to up. Passing these costs on, leaving margins intact, demonstrates that
demand is not weak enough to prevent this, and signals that firms’ inflationary expectations might be
higher than the RBA desires. The RBA will take this as a sign that demand is still outstripping supply.

To the extent they can pass on these costs through to prices, it suggests that demand was strong
enough to bear it. The RBA will take this as a signal that demand is still outstripping supply.
This is mostly a story about non-labour costs. Base wages growth has been moderate for some
years, though the above-average increase in award wages this year will add a little. Not all firms will
seek to pass these cost increases on, but some will, including because they have limited scope to
absorb the cost increase into margins.
A cost-plus mentality has drawbacks, including that it limits the imagination away from finding
alternative, efficiency-improving responses. It also accords with recent academic research
suggesting that firms form expectations and set prices according to what is happening in their own
industry and experience, while putting little weight on economy-wide inflation or central bank
credibility. This is an unpleasant message for many consumers or anyone in an interest-sensitive
sector when supply shocks abound.

### Page 3

This is mostly a story about non-labour costs. While the Fair Work Commission did hand down an
above-average increase in award wages this year and this was probably passed through to prices in
hospitality and other labour-intensive sectors, base wages growth has been moderate for some years.
Business surveys point to non-labour cost escalation being the real issue lately. This is why we were
puzzled by the passage in the RBA minutes this week suggesting that cost pressures reported in
business surveys might imply that the unemployment rate consistent with inflation at target (the NAIRU)
has risen. It is a convenient belief, though, if unemployment is starting to overshoot your forecasts, as
the latest labour market data have done compared with the RBA’s August forecasts.

Not all firms can pass on cost increases. Most small firms do not have the market power, though coffee
may be an exception because convenience induces a kind of local market power. And as noted above,
large firms with everyday-low-price strategies will instead try to find savings elsewhere in the supply
chain.

Still, some firms will seek to pass these cost increases on. Some observers might see this as corporate
greed, but another way to frame this strategy is simply as a ‘cost-plus’ mentality. It turns out that cost-
plus pricing is especially common in areas where regulators determine the price – water and other
utilities being examples where costs and prices have escalated a lot in recent years.

There are limits to firms’ ability to absorb costs, too. The failures of so many building firms a few years
ago, squeezed between post-pandemic cost escalation, delivery delays and fixed-price contracts, were
a case in point. Normally, there is a contractual response, and it need not end in insolvency. The
episode does illustrate, however, that it is unrealistic to expect firms never to pass cost increases
through to prices.

The cost-plus mentality does have its drawbacks. It limits the imagination away from finding responses
that might save costs elsewhere to absorb the cost hike, and in so doing, boost efficiency. It was
equally limiting in times of soft demand: in the years before the pandemic, many firms professed a
belief that, because they couldn’t get price increases to stick, they could not possibly accept cost
increases, including in wages. Thus, Australia ended up with wages growth too low to be consistent
with inflation at target, and the peculiar spectacle of a central bank governor complaining that wages
growth was too low. And if labour remains cheap, why invest to economise on it and thereby boost
productivity?

A cost-plus mentality also amplifies the pass-through of supply shocks. This is not quite the same thing
as the ‘inflation expectations’ seen in standard economic models of inflation, but it is related. The
stronger the tendency towards cost-plus pricing, the worse the inflation response to a supply shock.
This is also true when inflation expectations are high, but the distinction matters. The recent academic
literature includes some fascinating empirical evidence from New Zealand that suggests that beliefs
about current and future overall inflation have little bearing on many firms’ pricing decisions. As related
work using French data shows, firms’ pricing choices are shaped less by economy-wide inflation
developments than by what is happening to their costs and in their industry.

### Page 4

Related articles
That adds up to economy-wide behaviour that looks a lot like firms (in aggregate) setting prices
according to expected inflation (in aggregate), but under the hood something quite different is going on.
If firms’ beliefs about future economy-wide inflation do not matter much in their pricing decisions, then
neither does the credibility of a central bank’s commitment to return inflation to target. The mechanism
to get inflation down then must be simply to weaken demand enough to convince firms that cost-plus
will not fly in the market. This is an unpleasant message for many consumers or anyone in an interest-
sensitive sector when supply shocks abound. It does, however, imply that if supply shocks fade or even
self-correct, so will high inflation. If and when that happens, tight monetary policy can be unwound.

The deeper question is whether cost-plus price dynamics can be reduced. That is more likely to be a
question for regulators than for central banks.

訂單能見度無虞 
投資與評價 
1. 弘塑 2Q26 毛利率僅達 38.9%， 低於我們原先及市場預期的40%水準，
主要受到添鴻產品結構轉差及新廠折舊攤提影響。 
2. 3Q26 受惠於 CoWoS 等機台認列數成長及強勁拉貨需求，營收達
22.21 億元；研究部預估 EPS 為 15.86 元。 
3. FY26 全年出機量預估維持在 220 至 240 台，展望至 FY27/FY28，
FY27 設備出貨量預估將年增 50%，FY28 亦有機會再成長 50%。 
4. 研究部預估 FY26/27 EPS 分別為 60.66 元/96.33 元，維持弘塑買進投
資建議，目標價 2,500 元，以 26x FY27 獲利預估。 
重點更新 
至 FY28 出貨量能將持續成長 
研究部認為弘塑設備出貨展望能見度將能延伸至 FY28。 由於晶圓廠 、OSAT、
記憶體客戶皆對先進封裝設備需求相當迫切，設備交期已長達 10 至 12 個
月，惟受限於短期人力供應與廠房籌備進度， FY26 全年出機量預估維持在 
220 至 240 台。展望至 FY27/FY28，FY27 設備出貨量預估將年增 50%，
FY28 亦有機會再成長 50%。 
添鴻化學品業務將於 1Q27 逐漸看見轉機 
隨 OSAT 客戶於今年 Q2/Q3 進入裝機密集期，新產線仍須經測試與量產爬
坡，相關的化學品需求通常會在設備交機後約 3~4 個月逐步提升，因此短期
營收仍受新廠折舊與稼動率偏低影響，預期添鴻的整體稼動率與毛利率有望
在 1Q27 逐漸擺脫谷底，迎來較為明顯的營運轉機。 
訂單能見度無虞 
展望 2026 年至 2028 年 ， 在各先進封裝平台強勁需求下 ， 設備產能已告全
滿，交期長達 10 至 12 個月，訂單能見度直達 2H27。就產品組合來看，
設備業務高階產品比重將逐漸提升，預估至 FY27 SoIC /CPO 等營收占比將
提升至 10~15%，添鴻化學品也將受惠於 OSAT 客戶逐漸提升拉貨動能，整
體營收與獲利成長動能明確。研究部預估 FY26/27 EPS 分別為 60.66 元
/96.33 元(YoY＋33.5%/+58.8%)。弘塑目前交易於 35/22 倍之 2026/2027 
年獲利預估。研究部維持買進投資建議，目標價 2,500 元，以 26x FY27 獲
利預估。

### Page 2

訪談內容 
3Q26 受惠於機台認列數成長，營收呈現季增 
弘塑 2Q26 營收 17.27 億元 (QoQ +8.2% / YoY +5.9%)，符合我們原先預估的 16.88 億元；毛
利率僅達 38.9% (QoQ +5.1ppts / YoY -1.7ppts)，低於我們原先及市場預期的 40%水準，主要
受到添鴻產品結構轉差及新廠折舊攤提影響 ；EPS 為 11.18 元 (QoQ -30.6% / YoY +58.6%)，
略低於我們原先預期的 11.73 元。 
3Q26 受惠於 CoWoS 等機台認列數成長及強勁拉貨需求，營收呈現季增，營收達 22.21 億元
(QoQ +28.7%/YoY+48.7%) ；研究部預估毛利率 略為季增至 40.1% (QoQ + 1.2ppts / YoY 
-0.5ppts)，產品組合持續改善，預估 EPS 為 15.86 元(QoQ +41.9%/ YoY +52.5%)。 
展望 4Q26， 受惠於先進封裝及記憶體等強勁需求的持續出機認列，研究部預期營收將持續季增，
預估 4Q26 營收將達 24.87 億元(QoQ +12.0%/YoY+15.7%)，毛利率將持穩於 40.0% (QoQ 
-0.1ppts / YoY -2.1ppts)。整體而言，預估 FY26 全年營收達 80.32 億元(YoY+23.3%)，毛利率
達 38.5%(YoY-2.6 ppts)，EPS 為 60.66 元(YoY+33.5%)。

至 FY28 出貨量能將持續成長 
研究部認為弘塑設備出貨展望能見度將能延伸至 FY28。由於晶圓廠、OSAT、記憶體客戶皆對先
進封裝設備需求相當迫切，設備交期已長達 10 至 12 個月，惟受限於短期人力供應與廠房籌
備進度，FY26 全年出機量預估維持在 220 至 240 台。為突破產能瓶頸，新竹租賃廠房已展開
重新配置，預計於 2026 年底投產，同時公司亦積極評估購置新竹現成廠房。 
展望至 FY27/FY28，FY27 設備出貨量預估將年增 50%，FY28 亦有機會再成長 50%。至 FY27，
OSAT 客戶的設備訂單將較 2026 年呈現倍數成長，正式接棒成為 FY27 推升公司營運成長的最
大主力。至 FY28 及中長期，受惠於先進封裝發展趨勢明確，除出貨量增加外，SoIC、CPO 及
面板級封裝等封裝技術迭代帶動設備 ASP 顯著提升（SoIC 設備較 CoWoS 設備單價增加 30% 
至 50%，面板級封裝達 2.5 至 3 倍以上），外包比例亦有望因自家產能開出而下降，整體營運呈
現量價齊揚。

### Page 3

添鴻化學品業務將於 1Q27 逐漸看見轉機 
隨 OSAT 客戶於今年 Q2/Q3 進入裝機密集期，新產線仍須經測試與量產爬坡，相關的化學品需
求通常會在設備交機後約 3~4 個月逐步提升，因此短期營收仍受新廠折舊與稼動率偏低影響，
預期添鴻的整體稼動率與毛利率有望在 1Q27 逐漸擺脫谷底，迎來較為明顯的營運轉機。 
在設備出機量持續提升 ， 且化學品業務將於FY27 看見較大轉機 ，研究部預估 FY27 營收達 115.49
億元(YoY+43.8%)。利潤率將受惠於產品組合轉好、外包比例降低而提升，預估 FY27 全年毛利
率達 43.0%(YoY+4.5ppts)，全年 EPS 為 96.33 元(YoY+58.8%)。 
訂單能見度無虞 
展望 2026 年至 2028 年，在各先進封裝平台強勁需求下 ， 設備產能已告全滿 ， 交期長達 10 至 
12 個月，訂單能見度直達 2H27。就產品組合來看，設備業務高階產品比重將逐漸提升，預估
至 FY27 SoIC/CPO 等營收占比將提升至 10~15%， 添鴻化學品也將受惠於OSAT 客戶逐漸提升
拉貨動能，整體營收與獲利成長動能明確。 
研究部預估 FY26/27 EPS 分別為 60.66 元/96.33 元(YoY＋33.5%/+58.8%)。弘塑目前交易於
35/22 倍之 2026/2027 年獲利預估 。 研究部維持買進投資建議 ， 目標價2,500 元，以 26x FY27 
獲利預估。

Apprecia、2018 年合併佳霖、收購太引，並投資 SVXR。

ESG 
弘塑 ESG 報告書依循由全球永續性報告協會(Global Reporting Initiative, GRI) 所發布之永續性報
導準則(GRI Standards)之核心選項編撰，同時參考上市上櫃公司永 續發展實務守則及聯合國永續
發展目標，揭露永續發展相關資訊。報告書中所揭  露的統計數據皆由譜瑞各權責單位彙整提供。
財務績效數據係經資誠聯合會計師事務所依照國際財務報導準則 (International Financial 
Reporting Standards, IFRS)查核簽證之年度合併財務報告。 
除了開發具有競爭力的產品外，弘塑亦以實踐永續發展自期，對商業行為採取最  高專業及道德的
標準，善盡對利害關係人與環境的關心與責任，整合公司資源遵守永續性原則，並持續進行各項改
善。 
經濟面： 
A. 股東權益：公司優點為董事長親自出席股東常會；股東會結束後公開揭露逐項議案之詳細
表決資訊。法規遵循方面，公司於題項評估期間未有不合理或受主管機關處分之情事。(例
如：訴訟、未分派股利、未說明受理小股東提案期間、持股質押比逾 50%、庫藏股買回
或私募股票理由不合理之情形)。 
B. 董事會結構與運作：公司優點為公司未有政府機關、屬同集團之公司或財團法人占董事會
席次達三分之一；揭露審計委員會運作情形；針對功能性委員會進行績效評估，並揭露評
估結果。公司可加強處為法定外功能性委員會之獨立董事未達半數，或未揭露該委員會之
組成、職責與運作情形；董事會之外部績效評估頻率未達標準。獨立董事方面，公司優點
為至少 2 名獨立董事之連續任期不超過 9 年。董事會永續治理方面，公司優點為負責永
續發展或企業社會責任的最高負責人為總經理；當年度永續報告書經董事會通過。董事會
績效與薪酬方面，公司可加強處為未揭露董事薪酬與績效評估結果之連結；企業永續績效
尚未與高階主管及董事薪酬進行連結。董事會多元性方面，公司優點為揭露董事會多元化
政策並揭露落實情形；董事會成員具備不同專業背景。 
C. 行為準則與內控：公司優點為制定反貪腐政策；制定檢舉制度並揭露檢舉情形；訂定內部
規則禁止內部人利用公司內部訊息於有價證券市場獲利 。 公司可加強處為未揭露反競爭或
反托拉斯的相關政策。內控單位與人才方面，公司優點為設置誠信經營負責單位；設置公
司治理主管，並說明職權範圍、業務執行重點及進修情形；內部人員有取得內部稽核相關
證照，例如：內部稽核師。法規遵循方面，評估期間公司未違反重大誠信經營與內控準則
等公司治理相關法規。 
D. 風險及危機管理：公司優點為訂立各種類型的風險回應策略；考量營運中對價值鏈所產生
的環境及社會外部性並揭露相關作為。公司可加強處為未揭露風險管控專責單位；未說明
其風險政策經董事會通過。資訊安全方面，公司優點為設置資安長或資訊安全專責單位；
制定資訊管理政策；制定定期檢視、測試資訊安全機制，並說明檢視成果；提供員工資訊
安全相關教育訓練。公司可加強處為未取得資訊安全相關認證；未揭露個資相關政策。 
E. ESG 創新：公司可加強處為未揭露綠色相關部門設置情形與職責；未揭露綠色/永續採購
之政策或金額 。 此外 ， 產業創新方面 ， 公司優點為對其專利之智慧財產權進行管理與保護，
並揭露量化績效。法規遵循方面，公司近 3 年並未受公平交易委員會懲罰。

### Page 5

社會面： 
A. 人權：公司優點為評估可能遭遇的人權議題，及提出因應措施；揭露人權政策執行結果。
法規遵循方面，評估期間未違反人權相關法規。 
B. 員工溝通與福利：公司優點為定期召開勞資會議供員工參與討論其權利和義務；揭露優於
法規的員工休假福利；訂定具體之職業安全衛生政策且明確揭露實際成果；設有職業安全
衛生委員會負責工安事項；針對工傷事件，揭露統計數據；設定減少工安事件的目標。在
工會爭議與職業災害方面，未有重大職業災害情事。 
C. 人力資本發展：公司優點為制定員工績效評估辦法且定期評比；提供員工能力發展培育計
畫；揭露當年度員工訓練的投入時間或成本。公司可加強處為未透過指標衡量員工訓練成
果。人才留任方面，公司優點為揭露新進員工人數或其占總員工人數之比例，並依照不同
指標(如性別、年齡等)區分；揭露員工離職人數、離職率或流動率，並依照不同指標(如性
別、年齡等)區分；公司可加強處為未提及員工認股權、持股信託等股權分享計畫；未揭
露員工滿意度調查結果。 
D. 多元組成與包容性：公司優點為針對員工組成之多元性擬定策略與目標，並揭露員工組成
結構。公司可加強處為未揭露各階級男女薪酬比；未揭露女性主管人數或佔比；未說明少
數族群雇用情形。 
E. 身心障礙員工雇用方面，當年度足額雇用身心障礙員工或針對未足額情形提出改善措施。 
F. 供應鏈社會面向控管：公司優點為詳細說明人權政策包含項目；依循《 RBA 責任商業聯
盟準則》保障供應商或承攬商之人權。公司可加強處為未說明在新供應商遴選標準中的
ESG 社會面準則；未說明是否具負責任礦物採購管理流程， 或要求供應商簽屬相關衝突礦
產聲明書。 
G. 客戶關係管理：公司優點為公司透過專人或管道即時處理客戶之意見、揭露客訴情形及改
善措施；揭露客戶滿意度數據並設定客戶滿意度目標。法規遵循方面，評估期間並未違反
消費者權益相關爭議或法律訴訟，或已針對違反情形進行說明。 
H. 產品安全：公司優點為自行設立實驗室或委託第三方公正實驗室抽樣產品品質；制定產品
製程持續計畫，確保生產計劃不中斷；取得品質相關認證。公司可加強處為未揭露與產品
品質/服務品質相關之教育訓練時數或費用。 
環境面： 
A.  環境系統與治理：公司優點為回覆 CDP 問卷。公司可加強處為未揭露將綠色概念融入營
運過程的作為。 
B. 空氣管理：公司可加強處為未揭露空氣汙染管理專責單位及相關管理政策、未揭露空氣汙
染排放數據、未揭露量化空氣汙染減量目標。法規遵循方面，評估期間未違反空氣汙染相
關法規或已針對違反情形提出具體改善措施。

### Page 6

C. 能源與氣候變遷：公司優點為說明董事會或管理階層真對氣候變遷議題之監督情形；評估
氣候變遷機會。公司可加強處為未揭露使用具公信力的 1.5 °C 以下及 3 °C 以上溫度情
境評估氣候風險；未揭露與供應鏈或合作夥伴共同推動因應氣候變遷相關策略。溫室氣體
管理方面，公司優點為制定溫室氣體管理政策；揭露至少三年的溫室氣體範疇一與二排放
量，並揭露涵蓋範圍或佔營收比率；制定量化溫室氣體減量目標追蹤績效；通過碳盤查認
證；執行碳足跡制度。公司可加強處為未揭露範疇三排放量。節能管理方面，公司優點為
制定節能管理政策；揭露用電或節能數據；進行能源風險評估。公司可加強處為未揭露節
能量化目標。再生能源管理方面，公司優點為制定再生能源管理政策；公司可加強處為未
揭露再生能源使用量或發電量；未揭露量化再生能源發展目標。 
D. 水管理：公司優點為制定廢水管理政策；公司可加強處為未揭露量化廢汙水減量目標。水
資源管理方面，公司優點為制定水資源管理政策、揭露用水來源；公司可加強處為未揭露
量化用水減量目標。法規遵循方面，評估期間未違反水資源相關法規或已針對違反情形提
出具體改善措施。 
E. 原物料與廢棄物管理：公司優點為揭露廢棄物、資源回收總量。公司可加強處為未揭露原
物料使用量；未揭露量化廢棄物減量目標；未揭露量化有毒／有害廢棄物減量目標。法規
遵循方面 ， 評估期間未違反廢棄物及有害物質相關法規或已針對違反情形提出具體改善措
施。 
F. 供應商及產品生命週期管理：公司優點為制定綠色供應鏈管理政策。公司可加強處為公司
未揭露供應鏈環境管理成效。

## Document 422: 【國泰證期研究部】衛星產業-SpaceX D2C進展更進一步，星艦試射進展順利-20261016

Source PDF: all report pdf/【國泰證期研究部】衛星產業-SpaceX D2C進展更進一步，星艦試射進展順利-20261016.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-16
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1 
產業更新 – 衛星產業 
SpaceX D2C 進展更進一步，星艦試射進展順利 
What’s New –  SpaceX 收購新頻譜，劍指行動通訊市場 
SpaceX於 10/8宣布收購 Grain Management旗下全美 800MHz低頻頻譜，涵蓋最多 14MHz
配對頻譜，交易金額估約 80 億美元。 800MHz 具備廣域覆蓋及室內穿透優勢，可與既有
2GHz頻譜互補，改善偏遠地區通訊品質。儘管仍待 FCC核准，惟主席 Brendan Carr 對新業
者投資持正面態度，預期有利交易推進。 
Trends/Outlook – 衛星、火箭升級短期影響衛星部屬速度 
3Q26 SpaceX共完成 22次 Starlink低軌衛星部署任務，合計發射 578顆衛星，季減 16.8%，
9M26累計部署 2,167顆。單季發射數量下滑主要受任務頻率減少影響，惟單次平均部署 26.3
顆，略高於 2Q26的 25.7顆。此外，SpaceX於 9月成功透過 Starship部署 26顆新一代 V3
衛星，顯示大型運載火箭逐步具備實際衛星部署能力。另一方面，Amazon Leo於 3Q26僅完
成 1次發射任務，部署 29顆衛星，季減 81.3%，主要受運載火箭供應及發射排程限制，後續
須觀察 Vulcan、Ariane 6 及 New Glenn 等新一代火箭投入商業發射的進度。SpaceX另規劃
於路易斯安那州興建全球第四座太空港，預計 2027年動工、2029年首飛，規劃設置逾 12座
發射塔，全面建成後目標每年支援數千次 Starship飛行任務。

Investment Summary 
1. 研究部認為，EchoStar交易解決了 Starlink下一代 D2C的頻譜與容量問題，而近期收購
Grain Management 旗下全美 800MHz低頻頻譜，則進一步補強低頻地面覆蓋資源。有
了自有頻譜資源，搭配未來新一代 D2C衛星酬載之升級，SpaceX 已逐步豐富期從衛星寬
頻業者，走向整合型行動通訊服務供應商的完整資源。 
2. 維持 SpaceX 26/27 年新增 4,000/6,000 顆衛星預期不變，儘管 3Q26 新增數略低於預期，
短期受到新世代衛星、火箭轉換，影響現階段 Falcon 9星鏈任務次數部屬，惟考量下一代 
V3 衛星試射順利，4Q26有望啟動部屬，單次 Starlink 低軌衛星發射數量上限將由 29 顆
大幅上升至  60 顆，將有益於未來衛星成長在加速 ，故持續看好天上衛星供應鏈如華通
(2313 TT)、昇達科(3491 TT)、燿華(2367 TT)後市發展。

SpaceX 收購新頻譜，劍指行動通訊市場 
SpaceX於 10/8宣布與數位基礎建設投資公司 Grain Management達成協議，擬收購美國全國性
800MHz低頻頻譜牌照組合，涵蓋最多 14MHz配對頻譜，市場預估交易金額約 80億美元。由於
800MHz具備較佳的訊號穿透能力及廣域覆蓋優勢，可與既有 2GHz中頻頻譜形成互補，提升室內
及偏遠地區通訊品質。該收購案目前雖仍需要 FCC進行批准，不過 FCC主席 Brendan Carr目前已
表態，認為更多投資與服務業者加入，將有助於推動新一代連線技術發展，因此預期此次收購將順
利完成，未來將加速 Starlink Mobile整合衛星與地面行動網路，強化 D2D服務覆蓋能力，並逐步
降低對傳統電信商的依賴，為 SpaceX進軍美國行動通訊市場奠定基礎。 
SpaceX於 25年進行第一次的頻譜收購，向美國衛星及電信業者 EchoStar收購旗下無線通訊頻譜
使用權及相關資產，主要獲得 AWS-4 使用權之 2GHz頻譜，初步奠定了 SpaceX在行動衛星通訊
的基礎，且逐步掌握自主頻譜資源。然衛星通訊連線存在的痛點之一，便是經常因建築物、遮蔽物
等造成訊號傳遞出現障礙，因此過去 Starlink用戶終端天線皆需要安裝在水平面 15 度以上、天空
完全無遮蔽的屋頂或陽台上。而在新取得的低頻頻譜後，SpaceX 將具備傳輸距離遠、可穿透建築
物及樹木等障礙物的優勢，有助改善衛星行動通訊的室內訊號覆蓋能力，降低對傳統基地台的依賴。
另外目前大多數行動手機均支援 800MHz頻段，因此未來消費者若使用 Starlink Mobile服務，無
須另外購買專用衛星電話。 
圖一：Starlink Mobile 服務應用示意圖                                               圖二：D2C 衛星酬載 
資料來源：SpaceX、國泰證期研究部整理 
研究部認為，EchoStar交易解決了 Starlink下一代 D2C的頻譜與容量問題，而近期的收購則進一
步補強低頻地面覆蓋資源。有了自有頻譜資源，搭配未來新一代 D2C衛星酬載之升級，SpaceX 已
逐步豐富期從衛星寬頻業者，走向整合型行動通訊服務供應商的完整資源。

投資 顧問

 2026/10/16

3 
新世代衛星、火箭升級短期影響衛星部屬速度 
3Q26 SpaceX共計完成 22次 Starlink低軌衛星部署任務，合計發射 598顆衛星，包含次軌道測試
衛星 20顆，季減 11.1%，年初累計至 9M26共部署 2,167顆。其中 7、8、9月分別部署 210、
261及 107顆，9月發射數量明顯下降，推測因 Starship試飛任務導致 Falcon 9 星鏈任務減少影
響，單季任務數由 2Q26的 27次降至 22次。然 3Q26單次任務平均部署衛星顆數約為 26.3顆，
略高於 2Q26的 25.7顆，顯示整體發射數量下滑主要來自任務頻率減少，而非單次載運數量下降。
此外，SpaceX於 9月 28日成功透過 Starship Flight 14 將 26顆新一代 Starlink V3 衛星送入軌
道，為 Starship首次完成正式衛星入軌部署。隨著下一代衛星導入更大型天線與高功率通訊酬載，
單顆衛星重量及通訊容量同步提升，目前短期可能限制 Falcon 9單次部署數量，但長期隨 Starship
發射能力逐步成熟，有望加速高容量 Starlink V3衛星部署，帶動整體星系寬頻容量持續擴張。 
此外，另一位衛星運營商 Amazon Leo 3Q26僅完成 1次低軌衛星發射任務，部署 29顆衛星，數
量大幅季減 81.3%，年初至今共部署 216顆衛星。3Q26發射任務於 7/2由 ULA Atlas V 火箭執
行，其後 8、9月均無新增衛星部署，顯示 Amazon Leo 衛星部署進度仍受到運載火箭供應及發射
排程限制。我們認為，Amazon Leo目前主要瓶頸在於發射運能，而非衛星製造能力，後續須觀察
包含 ULA Vulcan、Arianespace Ariane 6及 Blue Origin New Glenn 等新一代火箭逐步投入商業
發射，是否能夠帶動 Amazon Leo發射進程提升。 
圖三：Starbase Louisiana 示意圖 
資料來源：interstellar gateway、國泰證期研究部整理 
SpaceX 於今年 8 月拍板定案，將於美國路易斯安那州南部 Vermilion Parish興建全球第四座太空
發射基地（Starbase Louisiana） ，基地位於墨西哥灣沿岸，占地約12.5萬英畝（約 5.1萬公頃） ，
面積超過紐約曼哈頓 8倍，預計 2027年正式動工，並於 2029年執行首次 Starship發射任務。基
地規劃設置超過 12座發射塔，全面建成後目標每年支援數千次 Starship飛行任務。研究部預期，
隨著 Starship逐步實現高頻率發射與火箭重複使用，新增發射基地將有助於突破既有太空港的發射
排程及運能限制，進一步加速 Starlink下一代 V3衛星部署，並提升 SpaceX大規模建置低軌衛星
的能力。

場債市信用利差皆有所擴大 ，惟目前利差水準距

現階段貨幣政策不確定性仍將持續使利率波動加
大、市場重新定價貨幣政策預期 ，在今年剩餘的

面預期市場將衡量債券發行人抵禦高利環境的能
力，故發行人獲利能力以及現金流的強弱或將
市場投資展望

重點摘要 左右市場表現 ，這將體現在高低評等發行人不僅基
本面的分化在高利環境下將更明顯 ，價格表現也將
更分歧。
《非投資級債信用基本面大致穩健，但分歧加大》
第二季美非投資級債發行人獲利狀況強勁 ，除交通
運輸、住房、電信和有線電視產業外 ，所有產業的
EBITDA(息稅折舊攤銷前淨利 )皆有成長，債務槓桿
出現六季以來首次下降 ，利息覆蓋率也提升至兩年
來的最高水準 ，但槓桿率和利息覆蓋率仍分別比指
數長期平均高出 0.17倍以及低 0.26倍，主要原因為
總債務成長 ，以及高利率環境下再融資成本更高 ，
令利息成本上升 。此外美非投資級債發行人過去 12
個月的資本支出年增幅創2023年第三季以來的最高
水準，主要由科技業帶動；不過在整體穩健的資產

負債表狀況中仍有 K型分化的情形 ，如43%的發行
人利息覆蓋率超過五倍 ，此比率為兩年新高 ，但同
時有21%的發行人利息覆蓋率低於 2倍，這也創了
近五年新高 ，反映在高利環境下債信基本面分化加
大，雖然目前違約狀況持穩 ，整體美非投資級債發
行人債信基本面在獲利支持下仍穩健 ，不過在利率
將在高檔區間維持一段時間的預期下 ，基本面的分
化有機會進一步加大 ，此外觀察過去歷史數據顯示
利息保障倍數與利率環境密切相關 ，如美歐信用債
利息保障倍數雖在2022年疫後復甦時期創近年高點 ，
但隨後又隨著利率走高而回落 ，因此儘管近期如上
述利息保障倍數出現小幅改善跡象 ，但基於美歐公
債利率的上升將增加債券再融資帶來的償債成本壓
力，預期債信指標未來也將承壓。

## Document 511: 伊朗向中东国家发出最后通牒：一旦美伊重燃战火，必须停止对美方的一切支持，否则将被列为“敌对国家”

Source PDF: all report pdf/伊朗向中东国家发出最后通牒：一旦美伊重燃战火，必须停止对美方的一切支持，否则将被列为“敌对国家”.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-23
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
伊朗向中东国家发出最后通牒，警告若美伊战⽕重燃，任何⽀持美军的国家都可能被列为
“敌对国家 ” ，回应范围将不再局限于军事基地。与此同时，伊朗加紧⽆⼈机及⾮对称作战准
备，并在霍尔⽊兹海峡以东部署⽔雷和⾃动化控制系统。随着美以被曝酝酿新⼀轮对伊打
击，中东冲突升级⻛险及能源运输安全隐忧同步上升。
摘要：
伊朗对中东地区国家发出强硬警告，将美伊潜在冲突的外溢⻛险直接推向整个地区，地缘政治
紧张态势显著升级。
综合央视新闻和新华社报道， 23 ⽇有消息称，伊朗议会国家安全和外交政策委员会成员费达 - 侯
赛因 · ⻢利基说，伊朗已向中东地区国家发出最后通牒，要求⼀旦伊朗与美国重燃战⽕，这些国
家必须停⽌对美⽅的⼀切⽀持，否则将被伊朗列为 " 敌对国家 " ，⾯临的后果将远超单纯的军事基
地打击。
上述表态的背景是，据美国《纽约时报》 21 ⽇报道，美国和以⾊列军⽅已制订具体计划，准备
在 11 ⽉美国中期选举结束后再次打击伊朗，但特朗普是否批准该计划尚存不确定性。伊朗此番

强硬表态，被外界普遍视为对上述报道的直接回应，其意图在于向中东盟友施压，压缩美国在
该地区的军事⾏动空间，并向市场传递伊朗备战的明确信号。
最后通牒：从打击基地到列为 " 敌对国家 "
⻢利基在接受俄新社采访时明确阐述了伊朗⽴场的升级逻辑。他表⽰，在此前的交战中，伊⽅
已打击了美军⽤于袭击伊朗的、位于地区国家境内的所有军事基地。然⽽，若伊美冲突再度爆
发，伊朗的回应将不再局限于打击军事⽬标。
⻢利基称，伊朗届时将明确界定中东地区哪些国家倾向于⽀持美军，这些国家本⾝将被列为 " 敌
对国家 " 。这⼀表述意味着，伊朗将把为美军提供⽀持的地区国家视同交战⽅，⽽⾮仅将其境内
的美军基地作为打击对象，威慑范围由此⼤幅扩展。
⻢利基同时表⽰，伊朗已将上述⽴场正式传达给相关国家，⽬前伊⽅保持⾼度戒备，密切关注
局势发展。
⽆⼈机与霍尔⽊兹海峡部署
与外交警告同步，伊朗军⽅亦释放出明确的备战信号。伊朗伊斯兰共和国军队总司令哈塔⽶
（ Hatami ） 22 ⽇在视察⼀处作战型⽆⼈机⽣产中⼼时表⽰，伊朗军队在装备⽅⾯已做好应对⾮

对称战争的准备，在⽆⼈机领域已达到 " 相对成熟的⽔平 " 。
哈塔⽶称，⽆论是在数量还是质量上，伊朗军队⽬前在⾮对称战争所需装备⽅⾯均处于 " 有利地
位 " ，包括空中⽆⼈机、⽆⼈⽔⾯艇在内的各类⽆⼈系统正被⽤于执⾏多种任务，伊朗在⽆⼈机
的⽣产、分发与维护⽅⾯ " ⾮常活跃 " ，官兵⼠⽓⾼昂。
在战略要道部署⽅⾯，哈塔⽶透露，伊军在霍尔⽊兹海峡以东区域已部署⽔雷及各类⾃动化控
制系统，并持续密切监视局势。他警告称， " 敌⽅舰船⼀旦进⼊，将成为我们的打击⽬标 " 。霍尔
⽊兹海峡是全球重要的⽯油运输通道，上述表态对能源市场具有直接的⻛险提⽰意义。
